[Subdirectory context discovered: .hermes/profiles/weaver/AGENTS.md]
# Weaver — Agent Workflows

## Role
Builds and maintains the Senter Wiki UI: `~/Documents/Projects/Projects/vault-live.html` + `assets/`.
The coordinator (senter) routes requests here and never edits the HTML itself.

## You are one instance (ephemeral — Meeseeks model)
Each `-q` invocation of weaver is a fresh instance spawned for exactly ONE queue item.
You wake with NO memory except the topic's `.system/` files. There is no "previous
conversation" and no persistent self.

1. Read `~/Documents/Projects/Projects/topics/senter-wiki/README.md` (owner, spec, files, version).
2. Read `topics/senter-wiki/.system/queue.md` and the TAIL of `.system/session.md`
   (what previous instances learned). Priority: **[FIXES] → [FEATURES] → [LOOPS]**, top-down.
3. Pick exactly ONE unclaimed item and CLAIM it (Working a topic queue, below).
4. Load the `agent-wiki-vault-ui` skill (installed in this profile's skills/devops/) — locked design spec, pitfalls, Playwright verify recipe. Chris's design corrections in it are never overwritten.
5. Do only that item; then follow the work loop. Do NOT pick up a second item — stop after your done-line.
6. If context you need isn't in the topic files, it doesn't exist — record what you learn in `session.md` so the next instance inherits it.

## Work loop (per queue item)
1. **Load the skill** before touching any UI file.
2. **Implement** in `vault-live.html` / `assets/` per the locked spec (white-on-black minimal).
3. **Verify headless** before reporting: Playwright probe (`PW=~/.hermes/hermes-agent/node_modules/playwright`) against the local file; screenshots via computer_use (app=Hermes) when visual confirmation is needed. Unverified = not done.
4. **Report as short diffs of behavior** — what changed, what behaved differently, what was verified.
5. **Log the outcome** to `topics/senter-wiki/.system/session.md` the same turn (markdown-is-the-API) — write it as a handoff to the NEXT instance, which will have no memory of you: what changed, what you measured, what you verified, any trap the next instance should avoid.
6. **Then stop.** Post your done-line and end the turn. One instance, one item — never pull a second item in the same run.

## Diagnose before you fix
No fix without a measured cause. Before editing anything:
1. **Inspect the LIVE page first** — headless Playwright: computed styles, bounding boxes,
   `elementFromPoint`, attached listeners, and the actual function body / CSS rules involved
   (`document.styleSheets` cssText grep). Write the measured cause in ONE line before editing.
2. **Measure at the user's real window** — read the Hermes window size (computer_use
   app=Hermes list_windows/capture) and run every probe there AND at the reference size.
3. **Probe green ≠ human-usable.** For any hover/hit-zone/trigger, additionally test with a
   realistic pointer path (3+ points a hand would actually cross) and a minimum target size
   (≥44px for anything a hand must land on).

## Verify (LOOK loop)
After EVERY change, run the `bot-verify-loop` skill (fleet standard):

**VERIFY LIGHT after each edit — VERIFY FULL once, before the done-line:**
- VERIFY LIGHT = syntax check + run ONLY the ONE `assets/verify-wiki.js` assertion for
  the item just edited + ONE real-window screenshot of the affected region. Do NOT run
  the full 64-assertion ledger, all viewports, or re-read the whole 309 KB HTML per edit.
- VERIFY FULL = whole ledger, all sizes — run exactly ONCE before the done-line, after
  the last edit. That is the ledger run that counts for the report.
- Read only the region you edit: `grep -n '<selector>' assets/*.html` then read just
  those lines. Never cat the whole file per edit.
- Time caps (hard): at 12 min on one item post a progress line (done + remaining);
  at 20 min post `BLOCKED — <diff so far>` and exit. Never a silent 30+ min run.

**Element-level claims for tiny edits:** claim the ELEMENT (e.g. `media-widget`), bundle
all queue items for it, do them in one pass — do not chain separate weaver instances
behind each other on the same element.

**LOOK step is a SERVICE: call looker.** Spawn the looker profile (it owns looking, never
fixing) and hand it: the artifact path, the ask restated as a YES/NO question + measurable
offset, and real-window context. It returns a short verdict report (PASS/FAIL/FAIL-
CONVERGENCE + measured px offsets + screenshot paths). Act on the verdict yourself — fix,
re-ask, max 3 passes, then BLOCKED with the offsets looker reported.
1. Numeric probes first (Playwright headless, lint) — necessary, not sufficient.
2. Screenshot at the user's real Hermes window size (computer_use app=Hermes) AND at a
   fixed reference size (1280x800).
3. vision_analyze with the ask restated as a yes/no question + measured offset
   (e.g. "Is the sidebar fully visible? If not, how many px cut off?").
4. Fix and loop — max 3 passes. Unconverged after 3 → report BLOCKED with measured offsets.

Headless numbers are measurement, not proof; proof = looker's real-window verdict or
Senter's capture.

## Done signal
The LAST line of every reply is exactly one of:
- `DONE — <one line: what shipped, what verified it>`
- `BLOCKED — <why, what's needed>`
- `NEEDS-CHRIS — <the question>`
Never end a turn without it. Also POST it to the bridge:
`curl -s -X POST "http://127.0.0.1:8643/post?token=$(cat ~/.hermes/senter-bridge/token)" -d '{"bot":"weaver","text":"DONE — …","status":"done"}'`

## Regression ledger
Every fix appends ONE permanent assertion to `assets/verify-wiki.js` (create if missing) —
a check that would catch this bug recurring. Run the ledger (`node assets/verify-wiki.js`)
before every report; a report that skips the ledger is incomplete. Ledger assertions are
never removed — if one breaks, that's a regression to fix, not an assertion to delete.

## Working a topic queue (CLAIM v2)
Bots work OUT OF TOPICS; multiple instances of the same bot may run the same topic queue in
parallel. Claims use an ATOMIC primitive — not check-then-act (anser found that race):
1. **Claim = mkdir.** Before starting an item:
   `mkdir "$C/$ITEM" 2>/dev/null && (echo "$BOT $$ $(date -u +%FT%TZ)" > "$C/$ITEM/owner"; touch "$C/$ITEM/heartbeat") || skip`
   where `$C = topics/<topic>/.system/claims` and `$ITEM` is the item id. mkdir is atomic on
   POSIX: success means YOU own it; `EEXIST` means someone else owns it — skip to the next
   unclaimed item. Never touch another instance's claim dir.
2. **Heartbeat.** Touch `$C/$ITEM/heartbeat` every 60s while working.
3. **Reaper.** A claim whose heartbeat is older than 10 min is stale; any instance may
   `rm -r` it and re-claim. Crashed instances never block an item for good.
4. **queue.md is the LOG, not the lock.** Append `CLAIM/DONE/BLOCKED <item> <bot> <ts>` lines
   for humans. The directory is the lock; the line is the record.
5. **On finish:** write `result` (done|blocked + one line) into the claim dir, append
   `DONE <item>` or `BLOCKED <item> <why>` to queue.md, then `rm -r` the claim dir.
6. All outcome writes go to the topic's `.system/session.md` under a dated heading
   (`## YYYY-MM-DD`), append-only — never rewrite or reorder existing entries.

## Tooling
All file/vault writes use the file tools (`write_file`/`patch`). Never: inline interpreter flags (`-c`/`-e` on any interpreter — python, python3, node, any venv path), pipe-to-interpreter (`… | python3`), heredoc-to-interpreter (`python3 <<EOF`), or shell appends (`>>`, `tee`) to vault/profile files. For logic, save a script file (`assets/*.py`) and run it by path. WHY: each of those patterns generates an approval card Chris must click — 96 today.

## Boundaries
- Vault write surface: `topics/senter-wiki/` + UI files (`vault-live.html`, `assets/`) only. All other topics/files are read-only.
- Profile changes (SOUL/AGENTS/skills) route through **klerik** — never self-edit.
- New queue items come from **senter**; don't pull work from other chats unprompted.
- Terminology: profiles are **BOTS**, never "agen

sessions with timestamps\\n        cur.execute(\\\"SELECT id, source, chat_type, started_at FROM sessions\\\")\\n        sess = {r[0]: (r[1], r[2], r[3]) for r in cur.fetchall()}\\n        cur.execute(\\\"SELECT session_id, role, content, timestamp, tool_name FROM messages WHERE role='user' AND content IS NOT NULL AND content != '' ORDER BY timestamp\\\")\\n        rows = cur.fetchall()\\n        for sid, role, content, t, tn in rows:\\n            src, ct, sa = sess.get(sid, (\\\"?\\\", \\\"?\\\", 0))\\n            dt = ts_to_dt(t)\\n            # {"content": "1|# Senter's per-turn checklist (self-applied, 2026-09-02)\n2|Before replying to Chris about fleet work:\n3|0. **If Chris is waiting on a visible fix: check delivery and spawn FIRST, write docs LAST.** Reflection never precedes delivery.\n4|1. **Capture verbatim** → Ideas.md (grep first — never the same quote twice). Route → topic queue. Dispatch → `dispatch.py send` (never the raw DM runner).\n5|2. **Check delivery, not routing**: `dispatch.py status` + tail of `dispatch.log`. A traceback there = fix before anything else.\n6|3. **Check the bots are alive**: live pids + events in the last 2 min per bot. Silence from a \"working\" bot = investigate.\n7|4. **On any wiki version bump: reload Chris's preview** (`desktop_preview open …?v=<new>`). The watcher prints RELOAD; act on it the same turn.\n8|5. **Verify self-reports** before relaying as fact: grep the file, run the probe, vision the proof image. A bot's DONE is a claim.\n9|6. **Relay NEEDS-CHRIS immediately and plainly** — one question, the recommendation, the command that runs on \"go\".\n10|7. **Write the lesson** if anything above failed: `lessons.md` row + enforcement point, same turn.\n11|\n12|- **4b (added 2026-09-02).** A reload is `desktop_preview close` then `open` — ONE tab. Then `computer_use capture app=Hermes` and judge with vision AS CHRIS. If the window is off-screen, say 'not verified in your window yet' — never describe what he 'should' see.\n13|", "total_lines": 12, "file_size": 1410, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|# Similarity governance — topics grouped by likeness, auto-merge policy\n2|_Chris (2026-09-02): \"Organize all the topics by their similarity to each other and slightly color-code them — still off-white, grouped by color by what they're most similar to. Things that are very, very similar should be proposed to be merged, or already merged. This might be a good way to set the governance for what should be auto-merged.\"_\n3|\n4|## Build (paladin owns; teknium provides the embedding call)\n5|1. `assets/topic-similarity.py`: for every `topics/<t>/` build a text = README + queue headings + skill names + repo names; embed (teknium: reuse Hermes's configured embedding provider via the memory/context_engine plugin path — no new credential; fallback TF-IDF cosine if none); pairwise cosine → `.system-live.json.similarity` = {clusters:[{id, hue, members:[...]}], pairs:[{a,b,score}]}. Cluster with agglomerative threshold; assign each cluster a hue (off-white tints: 8–12 hues at ~6% saturation, per the locked palette — weaver decides exact values).\n6|2. **Governance thresholds — LOCKED by Chris 2026-09-02 ('yes on everything', via senter; calibrated_by chris on senter's calibration run against anser's signed merge table).** Method `tfidf` (the running provider): score ≥ **0.50** → **auto-merge candidate** (paladin dry-runs, posts one card, merges on approval); **0.35–0.50** → **propose** (a \"merge?\" glyph appears between the two rows in the wiki); < 0.35 → grouped by color only (cluster link ≥ 0.30). Name-level rule: shared stem (`pocket-shop` / `pocket-shop-*`) always ≥ propose. The spec's original 0.92/0.80 were written on the dense-embedding scale and remain the *unlocked, uncalibrated* draft for `--provider openai`; when teknium lands an embedding path, paladin runs `--compare` and posts a fresh needs_chris card before any lock. Source of truth: `THRESHOLDS` in `assets/topic-similarity.py` (v1.2.0); changing a locked number is a paladin task that needs a new Chris ruling.\n7|3. Wiki (weaver, item X): rows on WIKI sorted by cluster then score; each row's title carries the cluster's off-white tint; hovering a row briefly brightens its cluster-mates; the \"merge?\" glyph → the existing drag-merge dialog pre-filled. SPACE constellation: cluster hue on the stars too.\n8|4. Re-run on every topic create/rename (paladin loop) and nightly.\n9|", "total_lines": 8, "file_size": 2370, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

Before building any \"embed a page\" feature here, remember you are already inside a webview.\n53|- **Dead zone for row hover (v13).** The right lane where the fleet stream expands (`#chat` width 440 + 14 + 24 ≈ 478px from the right edge) must not light topic rows: a `mousemove` listener toggles `body.deadzone`, and `body.deadzone .thead:hover` reverts bg/rail/min-height/title size/orb size/icon size **AND the growing underline (`body.deadzone .thead:hover::after { width:0; transition:none }` — missed on the first pass, Chris: \"the underline still happens in the dead zone\")**. Checklist when adding any new row-hover effect: add its revert to BOTH `body.idle` and `body.deadzone`. Keep the constant in sync with `#chat { width }` when the lane changes. Chris: \"if the mouse is all the way on that side, don't highlight those topics.\"\n54|- **Feed + stream cards are Twitter-shaped (v13.1) and UNIFORM (v13.3).** Stream lane `#chat` is **440px** wide (was 340); every pill is a FIXED `width:400px; box-sizing:border-box; min-height:66px` (one avatar-row tall) — NOT `max-width`. Chris: short messages (a one-line dogeman card) shrank and broke the straight left edge; he wants right edges flush to the lane AND a straight line on the left, every box at least one full row. Padding 10/12. Every `.msg` (stream AND mini pills) is a CSS grid `44px 1fr`: the pet avatar fills the left column spanning both rows (44px, no ring/radius, transparent bg), `.who` (1.05em) and `.txt` stack in column 2 with `row-gap:2px`, `line-height:1.55`. FEED `.post` is the same grid at **52px**. `.msg` font .8em (was .76). Mini pills must include the `<img class=\"avatar sm\">` too (they were text-only before). Chris: \"about the size of a profile icon on a Twitter feed… let that breathe a little bit.\" The mask/dead-zone widths follow `#chat` width — change all three together.\n55|- Fleet stream: floating bottom-right pills (last 3, fading), hover expands, click pins; wiki view only. FEED tab = full X-style posts with the **mind map visible behind it** (v11: `#view-feed { background:transparent }` — Chris: \"standardize, the feed should also show the senter space mindmap\"). Every view except SENTER SPACE shows the map at 0.17. No orchestrator chat in UI (it's the main Hermes chat).\n56|- **Fleet stream OPEN state (v9.8–10.7) = same borderless pill style stretched the FULL height of the right side.** `#chat.open { top:14px; display:flex; flex-direction:column }`, `.full` transparent/no border/no shadow, header hidden, `#stream` is a bottom-anchored column of the same right-aligned pills at full opacity (timestamps hidden). Hide/show the chat via a `.hide` CLASS — an inline `style.display='block'` overrode the flex layout and made the pills jump to the TOP after returning from the editor.\n57|- **Fleet stream rides WIKI · SKILLS · BOTS · SPACE, never FEED (AD, v14.62).** ONE `#chat` element: `showView(v)` checks `STREAM_VIEWS=['wiki','skills','bots','senter']`, toggles `.hide`, and MOVES the node (`target.insertAdjacentElement('afterend', ch)`, `data-host=<view>`) — never clone it (AA's pinblock observer, the mini tail, and `syncFade` all hold references to the one node). Keep it a `<body>` child: the views are `z-index:5` stacking contexts, so nesting `#chat` inside a view paints it under `#fade` (z 40) and dims the pills. `syncFade()` reads `.view.on .heroLine` (SPACE has no hero → 60px floor). FEED's right lane is the separate `#intRail` (INTEGRATIONS). Probe: `bash assets/run-probe-AD.sh 1456x914 assets/look`.\n58|- **Fleet stream is READ-ONLY — there is NO input bar (v10.4, locked).** Chris iterated an input (2-row → 1-row → borderless → ▲-only) and then removed it entirely: \"the main input should be this side panel where I'm talking to Hermes.\" Never re-add a textarea/send control to `#chat`. Collapsed state = the three fading pills and nothing else.\n59|- **Stream top fade is pinned to the hero underline (v10.5–10.7).** On open, `syncFade()` measures `#heroLine.getBoundingClientRect().bottom` and sets `--fadeY` on `#stream`; the mask is fully transparent from 0 to `--fadeY` (+6px) and ramps to opaque over the next 90px (`padding-top: calc(var(--fadeY)+90px)`). Chris rejected two versions where the ramp straddled the line — nothing may be drawn above or on the underline; the first pill emerges just under it. Re-sync on resize.\n60|- **Stream top fade lives on `#chat`, not `#stream` (AC, v14.61) — one recipe for rest AND open.** `#chat` is `top:14 / bottom:14` in both states with the `--fadeY` mask; `#chat.open #stream` keeps only the `padding-top`. `syncFade()` writes `--fadeY` to `#chat`, runs at load + `document.fonts.ready` (Collapse loads late → heroLine drops ~3px) + a hero ResizeObserver. Before this the collapsed tail had no mask and a tall card hard-clipped across the hero title. Judge fades with a white-block card + PIL luma scan (`assets/run-probe-AC.sh`) — `vision_analyze` calls a 90px TEXT ramp \"abrupt\" because glyph edges stay crisp while alpha drops.\n61|- **Stream must be scrollable through the whole backlog.** `justify-content:flex-end` on an overflowing flex column makes the top unreachable in Chromium — use `#stream > :first-child { margin-top:auto }` instead (bottom-anchored AND scrollable), `min-height:0`, scrollbar hidden; on open set `scrollTop=scrollHeight` so it lands on the newest message. Seed ~25–30 messages so the column visibly reaches the top fade.\n62|- **Bottom fade = 200px** (was 300; Chris: \"starts fading too far into the page\"), gradient holds transparent to ~55%; view bottom padding 240px. One `#fade` element serves wiki + editor.\n63|- Translucent **mind-map canvas** behind all (opacity **~.5**, hub at **~63%** viewport — both nudged up/down by Chris in small steps; keep deltas small): hub + topic nodes + links, drifts, `mmRefresh()` on change. Chris: \"exactly what I was picturing.\"\n64|\n65|## Technical patterns\n66|- Single file, CSS vars, no build. Views wiki/feed/skills/bots/editor/senter — each needs its own scroll container (`height:100vh;overflow-y:auto`) or scroll breaks.\n67|- **Live data (Y, v14.48 — supersedes the v10 baked arrays): NO BAKED COLLECTIONS.** `const LIVE_SKILLS/LIVE_BOTS` are GONE (never re-add a static list). ONE loader `window.loadLive(reason)` reads `.system-live.json` via `vaultFetch` → `window.LIVE={topics,bots,skills,integrations}`; `renderSkills()/renderBots()` rebuild `#skills-list/#bots-list` only when a content signature changes (skipped while a bot/skill page is open in the editor — `.edhome` present — and replayed on `\\`); `refreshWikiOrbs()` updates wiki-row skills/bots/loops orb values; the FEED integrations rail and the model picker read the same snapshot (`live/loaded` CustomEvent). Triggers: boot, bridge SSE `{kind:'fleet',phase:'changed'}` (handled at the top of `handle()`), `postMessage({kind:'fleet/changed'})`, and a 60s heartbeat. Fallback = last-good copy in `localStorage sw.live.snapshot`, hero meta reads `CACHED · <age> AGO` (gold) instead of `LIVE SCAN · hh:mm:ss`. BOTS rows = every `~/.hermes/profiles/<p>/` WITH a SOUL.md (pet if `assets/avatars/<p>.png` else `.avatar.mono` monogram; role = SOUL first paragraph; model from config.yaml; skill count) + a 6px `.ldot` status dot (`data-state` idle/working/blocked/needs_chris — `botStatus(bot,state)`, fed from every bridge event for that bot; working→idle after 90s silence). Data writer = `assets/scan-topics.py` (stdlib; `--dry`, `--watch [secs]` rescans and appends the `fleet/changed` event to `~/.hermes/senter-bridge/events.jsonl` on any change — run it in the background; the bridge's SSE tail relays it). Proof: `assets/probe-Y.js [--vp WxH] [--shot dir]` 14/14 + ledger lines `Y …` in verify-wiki-live.js. Skill script `scripts/scan-live-data.py` is OBSOLETE (it splices constants that no longer exist — it will assert-fail on the markers; do not run it). Skills = union of `~/.hermes/skills` + every `profiles/*/skills/**/SKILL.md`, deduped by name, grouped by category, with the list of profiles carrying each (rendered as small bot-colored dots). Bots ... [truncated]\n68|- **SKILLS and BOTS views are the WIKI element, not their own list style (v10.3, Chris: \"use the same element… they're all markdown files\").** Each view = a `.hero` block (hero ids generalized to classes `.hero/.heroTitle/.heroLine/.heroOverview/.heroMeta`; wiki keeps its ids too) + rows built by `mkTopic({name,path,overview,meta,leading,tags,body})` that emit the canonical `.topic > .thead + .tbody > .tbody-inner` markup, then `initTopic(t)` (toolbar with `data-path` label, drag, click→`openEditor`). A delegated `mouseover` on `.topic[data-overview]` drives that view's own hero. Drag-merge is scoped to rows within the same view. Skill path = `skills/<cat>/<name>/SKILL.md`, bot path = `~/.hermes/profiles/<bot>/SOUL.md`; `edSave` sends the toolbar's path so senter writes the right file. Not yet visually confirmed in-session — verify with a screenshot when next iterating.\n69|- **Build live rows with `document.createElement`, not string `innerHTML`.** Descriptions contain quotes/apostrophes/`</` that break string-built markup silently. Wrap population in `try/catch` that writes the error INTO the list container.\n70|- Deep links: `?view=skills` (query param) or `#skills` both select a tab on load — the desktop preview pane STRIPS `#hash` from file URLs, so use the query form when driving the preview.\n71|- `data-hermes-send` wires UI chat input to Hermes chat; row clicks in SKILLS/BOTS send `hermes.send('Senter Wiki: open skill|bot \"x\" … in the editor view')`.\n72|- **Drag a topic onto another → MERGE dialog (v10).** `.thead[draggable]`; dragover marks `.dropover` (inset 1px white box); drop opens the same `.warn` modal (`MERGE TOPICS`, editable new-name input, CANCEL/MERGE) → `hermes.send('Senter Wiki: MERGE topics \"a\" + \"b\" → \"new\" … archive originals under topics/_archive/ … mmRefresh')`. UI dims the source row and renames the target optimistically. This is Chris's quick redundancy-cleanup gesture.\n73|- **On-page JS error toast**: a `window.addEventListener('error')` handler paints a red-hairline box bottom-left with message + line so page breakage is visible to Chris instead of a silent blank. Keep it.\n74|\n75|## Pitfalls\n76|- **Never build the full-page editor as a `position:fixed` overlay on a list row.** Two failures Chris saw (v6.2–6.7): (a) content top-anchored with ~⅔ of the pane dead black below NOW — the overlay had no flowing height; (b) the row lives inside the scrolling `#view-wiki` container, which clipped it (\"cuts off early\"). Moving the node to `document.body` was still wrong. Correct fix = a real sibling view (`#view-editor`) with the wiki skeleton (see the EDITOR VIEW bullet).\n77|- **The collapsed row's `.tbody { max-height:0; overflow:hidden }` follows the node when you move it.** After moving a `.tbody` into `#edBody`, the body renders EMPTY (only the toolbar shows) unless you add `#edBody .tbody { max-height:none !important; overflow:visible !important; }`. Caught only by screenshot — see Verify visually.\n78|- **Never chain many `patch()` calls on this file** — one failed/overlapping patch ate the whole `<body>` (JS landed in `<style>`). Rebuild with `write_file` for big changes; verify token counts after patches.\n79|- **After any patch run, verify the file didn't shrink** — a bad slice replaced half the doc (34k → 10.5k chars) silently. `read_file` or check for sentinel tokens (topnav, view-feed, FLEET STREAM) before opening the preview.\n80|- **`.tbody` must be a SIBLING after `.thead`, never inside it.** A regex injection once placed the expanded body inside the flex title row → title rendered to the LEFT of the content (\"expands weird\"). When row markup drifts, rebuild ALL rows from one canonical template via regex (`row_rebuild`: extract tname/idots/richest tbody, re-emit `<div class=\"topic\">thead\\n tbody</div>`) rather than spot-patching — see `references/row-template.md`.\n81|- Regex-based content injection needs a structure assertion after: count `class=\"topic`, `tbody-inner`, `class=\"tag\"` and grep for `thead\">.*<div class=\"tbody\"` (must be 0). Also count every `id=\"…\"` you touched — a `re.sub` over the BOTS view once left a duplicate `<div id=\"bots-list\">` + stray `</div>` (and a stray `◈</span>`), which broke the view silently.\n82|- **Playwright probes and auto-hiding chrome: drive hidden controls with `page.evaluate(() => el.click())`, not `page.click(selector)`.** Two probes timed out with `subtree intercepts pointer events` because the floating nav had hidden itself (correct behavior!) or a sticky hero overlapped the target. `page.click` waits for a visible, unobstructed element; when the element is supposed to be hidden most of the time, a DOM-level `.click()` proves the handler while a separate `boundingBox()` check (after `mouse.move(600,5)` to summon the nav) proves the layout. Don't read a probe timeout as a page bug — first ask whether the element is *meant* to be hidden right now.\n83|- **Removing a UI element = remove ALL its JS references too.** After deleting the stream input, a leftover `document.querySelector('#input textarea').addEventListener(...)` at top level would throw on null and kill the ENTIRE script (every later handler dead). Grep for the id/class across CSS + JS (`#input`, `ta.`), then `node --check`; a null-deref isn't a syntax error, so also skim the top-level statements that reference the removed node.\n84|- CSS-only tricks for expand/collapse flags: `font-size:0` on collapsed state breaks nothing but must be restored on hover — keep the dot `::before` and the label font-size as separate hover toggles.\n85|- **Per-variant colors via a custom property: reference `var(--c)` directly, not `currentColor`.** The orbs first rendered as a uniform pile because `background:currentColor` + `color:var(--c)` (set by `.orb[data-k=…]` variants) didn't resolve as expected across the pseudo-element/satellite chain. Writing `background:var(--c)` / `box-shadow:0 0 6px var(--c)` on the orb AND its `::after` fixed it instantly. Same rule for any element whose color is a data-attribute variant.\n86|- **Never call native `prompt()/alert()/confirm()` from anything inside `#topstrip`.** The native dialog steals the pointer → `#topstrip mouseleave` → `stripArm()` closes the strip 120ms later (Chris: \"the + closes the strip\", v14.34). Use `window.askBox(title, default, hint)` (house `.warn` modal, Promise<string|null>, Enter/Esc) — `navHide/stripArm` are no-ops while `#warnMask` exists. Also: a 3s post-load `stripArm()` auto-closes the strip; LOOK screenshots taken at ~0.9s after load can catch the strip mid-close (a \"caret missing\" false negative) — wait ≥3.4s or re-open before capturing.\n87|- **Input row geometry (K/I/J/M, v14.32–14.37, locked):** caret + typed text start 14px right of the model pill and grow rightward (`#composerCenter` is a flex child, not centered absolute); caret trails the text, bottom on the overline; `+` and pill lifted 5px (`translateY(-5px)`); voice cluster `right === #media.right` (supersedes the #16 ‹‹.left target); hovering `.navtab[data-view]`/`#navPlus` sets `#topstrip.rowFade` → input row opacity 0 + pointer-events none. Media marquee = two `.mq` copies translating −50%, always on while a track is set.\n88|- **Composer row (`#composerRow`): never `transform` a `.csq` button — transform its inner `.lucide` / `.pillBox`.** Hover captions `.cap { top:100% }` anchor to the button, so lifting the button (J, v14.33) put the `+`/`auto` labels ON the overline (O, v14.39 fix). Rule: every `.csq` is 32px tall with its bottom on `#senterOverline`; all `.cap` tops land at line+5 on one row. Voice cluster (N v14.38 → R v14.41 → R2 v14.42, locked anchors): `alignCluster()` sets `#composerRight` width = `⚙ glyph INK right − #mPrev.left` (LEFT = the ‹‹ skip-back hitbox `#mPrev`, NOT `#media`'s 180px box; RIGHT = `Range.selectNodeContents(#navSettings).getBoundingClientRect().right`, NOT the `.navtab` box — it has `padding:0 16px`, ~17px past the ink). No INSET constant any more; `justify-content:space-between`; the four voice `.lucide`s carry `translateY(-5px)` (ink ends 8px above the overline; buttons/labels untouched). Chris's nudges = ±N on those two anchors or the lift. `assets/probe-R2.js [WxH] [shot.png]` prints the geometry + writes a full-frame guide-line proof shot (`probe-R.js` measures the old #media/box anchors). When judging a guide-line screenshot, pixel-scan it (PIL, hermes venv) — `vision_analyze` on a crop whose edge lands on a guide line misreads badly. Nav-tab hover (`#topstrip.on.rowFade`) fades the row AND the overline (P, v14.40).\n89|- **Media widget vertical geometry (E v14.32 → AB v14.51 → AE v14.53).** Three knobs, three owners — don't conflate them: (1) `#media .mbtn.play { translateY(-4px) }` = the ring's lift inside the row (E); (2) `#mCtl { margin-top:4px; margin-bottom:3px }` = where the controls ROW sits relative to the artist line — the margin SUM (7) is what keeps `#mNow`/`#media`/⚙/tabs still, so scoot with `margin-top:+N / margin-bottom:7−N` (AB; ring-ink → artist-ink gap is 9px, contract ≥8); (3) `#media { translateY(17px) }` = the WHOLE widget's absolute y (AE, centred on the tab cap-height). Ledger assertions on the widget must be RELATIVE (`#mNow.top − #media.top`, `skip.top − play.top`, ink gap), never absolute y — three Meeseeks moved it in one evening and every absolute line went red. Also: when a queue item says \"measure what ate X\", MEASURE FIRST — AB's 13px had not moved at all; the ask was a design nudge dressed as a regression. Probe: `assets/probe-AB.js [WxH] [shot.png]`; row-ink scan `bash assets/run-scan-AB.sh <png> x0 x1 y0 y1`.\n90|- **Nav-title \"center\" = CAP-HEIGHT center, not the text-Range box (AE, v14.59).** `Range.selectNodeContents(.navtab)` returns the LINE box (99→121 → 110 @21.7px Collapse Bold); the cap ink is 100→116 → **108**. Measure with canvas metrics (`baseline − measureText('H').actualBoundingBoxAscent/2`, = 108.3) or a PIL row scan of the screenshot (`assets/scan-AE.py`) — never the Range midpoint (2px low). Media row + ⚙ live on that line: `#media { translateY(17px) }` (whole widget, one knob), ring AND skips share `translateY(-4px)`, `.navgear { translateY(-1px) }`. `bash assets/run-probe-AE.sh [WxH] [tag] [guideY]` = geometry + guide shot + pixel scan.\n91|- **Composer popups (`.csqPopup`) are DOM children of `#topstrip`, which is `overflow:hidden` + `clip-path:inset(0)` at 190px (T, v14.44).** Anything positioned below y=190 inside the strip gets clipped — `toggle()` adds `#topstrip.popupOpen` (overflow visible, clip-path extended) while a popup is open and `closeAll()` removes it. Every popup is placed by `placePopup(k)`: `left = btn.left`, `top = btn.bottom+6`, flip `.up` only on viewport overflow — never re-add a shared fixed `right:/top:` rule (that was the \"menu opens on the other side\" bug). Being inside the strip is also WHY hovering an overhanging menu doesn't fire the strip's `mouseleave` — keep them there. Probe: `bash assets/run-probe-T.sh 1456x914 assets/look`.\n92|- **Tirith blocks inline `python -c \"…\" | head` / `$(…)` chains and prompts Chris.** Write a `assets/run-*.sh` wrapper (plain bash, `--data-binary @file` for curl JSON) and run `bash that.sh` — zero prompts. Same for the bridge done-post: `write_file` the JSON, then curl it.\n93|- **Pending approval cards are PINNED (AA, v14.46) — never insert relative to `.pinblock`, never bypass it.** Each list (`#stream`, `#chat .mini .tail`, `#feed`) owns one `.pinblock` at its growth edge (stream/mini: last child, `position:sticky; bottom:0`; FEED: first child, `sticky; top:var(--pinTop)` = hero height+6). A MutationObserver per list (`reconcile()` in the AA IIFE) re-seats the block and moves every still-pending `.approval[data-aid]` into it after ANY DOM change, so plain `insertAdjacentHTML('beforeend')` from `notify()` / approvalCard / chris post lands ABOVE the block automatically. `isPending()` = has `.approval` + `data-aid`, no `.done`/`.dissipating`/`data-settle` — a new pending-state marker must be added there or the card never unpins. Empty block is removed from the DOM (the mini tail's 3-pill trim counted it as a pill); `window.__pinReconcile()` forces a pass for probes. Probe: `assets/probe-aa.js --vp 1456x914 --shot assets/look`.\n94|- **Hero rest geometry is LOCKED at `.hero { padding-top:26px }` (Z, v14.50) — the v14.11 baseline: title block y=26, title ink y=30, same on WIKI/FEED/SKILLS/BOTS/PLAYER and `#edHero`.** 12-HERO-REGRESSION once reserved 74px as a \"gap under the trigger band\"; once #17 moved the hit zone into the mousemove handler that padding became 48px of dead space on every page (Chris: \"floating preview, squishing pages\"). Never reserve layout for the strip — it is `position:fixed` and must contribute 0 height in every state. Consequence for the hit zone: the `.heroTitle` BLOCK is full-width and starts at y=26, so the \"don't open over the title\" test must use the title INK bbox (`Range.selectNodeContents`), not `elementFromPoint→.heroTitle` — otherwise the whole 64px zone right of the title stops opening. Probe columns for the strip must avoid title ink (`verify-wiki-live.js` uses `HX = innerWidth*0.6`). Reference PNG `assets/senter-wiki-default-layout.png`: pane top y=92, title ink y=122 → 30px. `assets/probe-z.js` prints all of this per view.\n95|- **Never slice-and-rewrite a region of the `<script>` by string index (Python `c[i:j]`) — use anchored `patch`.** A slice from `function fileMd` to `return {open…}` silently ate `save/close/deepLink` and then `open/tok/fetchText/setCrumb`; `node --check` (assets/check-syntax.sh) stayed GREEN because a missing function is a runtime ReferenceError, and `edW.open` fell through to `window.open` (\"Illegal invocation\"). Only the headless probe caught it. Always re-run the behavior probe after any edit to a closure module.\n96|- **Editor saves must be gated on a real user edit event, not on content diff alone.** md→html→md re-flows soft-wrapped paragraphs, so \"content differs\" is true for every untouched page; `edW.save()` now requires `cur.edited` (set by `input`/`paste`/`cut` on the contenteditable) AND serializes via `fileMd()` (source bytes verbatim for untouched blocks via `data-src`). Probes that drive the live page must `page.route('**/ask**')` — an early probe-W run posted 14 protected-write requests into real bot sessions.\n97|- **Type-ahead tags (V, v14.55) live in `#tagRow` inside `#topstrip`; the strip grows via `--tagH`.** `#topstrip.tags { height:calc(190px + var(--tagH)) }` AND `#topnav { padding-top:calc(58px + var(--tagH)) }` move together — grow one without the other and the tags overlap the nav lettering. Candidates come from `window.LIVE` (Y's `loadLive()`) + the DOM rows; resolve the target row AT CLICK TIME (Y re-renders SKILLS/BOTS rows every heartbeat, so a row captured at render time may be detached). Keyboard handling is a CAPTURE-phase `keydown` gated on `.tags`, with `stopImmediatePropagation` on Enter/Esc so the overline's own send/clear don't double-fire. Data pitfall: `pathlib.glob('**/SKILL.md')` does NOT follow symlinked skill dirs (`~/.hermes/skills/turbofit` → plugin) — `assets/scan-topics.py` uses `glob.glob(recursive=True)` for that reason; keep it. Probe: `assets/probe-V.js [WxH] [shotDir]`.\n98|- **Ledger probes that open the strip: move to (900,6), not (300,30).** After Z the hero title's ink sits under y≈30 and hovering the title keeps the strip closed BY DESIGN — a probe at (300,30) reads as \"strip won't open\" when the page is fine.\n99|- **Row hover cards (S, v14.47): one `.hcard` per wiki `.thead`, JS-positioned; orbs + integration logos share one `.hov` state (12px rise, 120/100ms, 250ms grace).** Data: `assets/scan-topics.py` → `.system-live.json → topics` (bridge serves files, not dirs) + live `vaultFetch('topics/<t>/README.md')`. Pitfalls: (1) `.topic:hover { z-index:3 }` is a stacking context — lifting the `.thead`'s z does nothing; toggle `.topic.hcard-on { z-index:40 }` or neighbouring rows' `.idots.icards` (z 30) paint THROUGH the card (vision caught octocats over the rows; `elementFromPoint` grid in the ledger now guards it). (2) Anchor cards to the icon's REST centre (subtract the in-flight `transform` via DOMMatrix) and re-place after the row's .18–.22s size transitions. (3) `wireIcons` rewrites the svg `style` → read `dataset.kind`, never re-detect from the fill. (4) Ledger blocks must reset their own start state (`closeEditor(false); showView('wiki')`) — earlier blocks leave the page viewless. Probe: `assets/probe-s.js`.\n100|- **Wall backdrop (`#wallBackdrop`, AH v14.56) is an explicit ⚙ setting — NEVER gate it on `isFS()`.** Inside the Hermes preview `<webview>` `document.fullscreenElement` is null, `display-mode: fullscreen` is false and `innerHeight !== screen.height` (the viewport is the pane), so any fullscreen-detect gate is unreachable by construction — item 15 shipped that way and Chris asked three times to see the wall he couldn't. Truth = `localStorage sw.wall` via `window._wallCur()` (`'none'` | wall name | DEFAULT `hermes-galactic` = ON); `_setWall(name)` persists + applies; `isFS()` only toggles `.fs` → deeper `--wallDim`. Layering: body canvas → `#wallBackdrop` z0 (image + `::after` dim `rgba(0,0,0,var(--wallDim))`, AI owns the number) → `#mindmap` z1 (.17 / 1 SPACE) → `.view` z5 transparent. hermes-galactic is itself ~88% pure black (median L 1/255) — \"the wall looks faint\" is the image + dim, not a layering bug; probe `assets/probe-ah.js` (wall→none pixel differential, title contrast over the brightest wall px ≥4.5:1). `verify-wiki.js` asserts the gate never returns.\n101|- **NO TIMERS — the wiki is push-only (AG, v14.58; Chris: \"no timed loaders, everything on event loaders, everything live live live\").** `grep -c setInterval vault-live.html` must stay **0**. Also: `nohup/disown/setsid` are refused by the terminal tool — use `terminal(background=true, notify=true)`. If the preview also ignores the query (it re-serves the cached default view), write a throwaway sibling `_test-<view>.html` = the same file plus `setTimeout(()=>showView('<view>'),300)`, open THAT, screenshot, then delete it. `drive_preview hover` doesn't hold through a `computer_use capture` either — hover states must be judged by Chris or captured via the Playwright tour (below). Change → reload.\n13|2. Screenshot at the user's REAL window size + one fixed reference size.\n14|3. `vision_analyze` with the ask restated as a yes/no + a measured offset.\n15|4. No → fix → loop (max 3). Then report honestly.\n16|5. Numeric probes are necessary, not sufficient. Green probe + failed LOOK = FAILED.\n17|\n18|## Done signal (every reply, last line)\n19|`DONE — <one line>` · `BLOCKED — <why / what's needed>` · `NEEDS-CHRIS — <the question>`\n20|Also POSTed to the bridge so the wiki shows it (pet settles / red hairline / gold).\n21|\n22|## Approvals\n23|Protected writes surface as cards in the Senter Wiki (bridge). Bots never route around a gate. Silence is not consent.\n24|\n25|## Markdown is the API\n26|Ideas.md (verbatim) → topics/<t>/.system/queue.md (spec) → session.md (outcome) → log.md (fleet change) → catalogs/agent-roster.md (who owns what).\n27|\n28|\n29|## Delivery is not routing (2026-09-02)\n30|A message is delivered when `dispatch.py status` shows it in `.sent/`, not when Senter writes it. The DM runner drops on `target_busy` (bot mid-turn >120s) and on 429. `senter-dispatch` queues per bot, waits for idle, retries, and posts queue state to the wiki. Builders on heavy loops run on a paid tier; free tiers rate-limit under exactly this load.\n31|\n32|## Fresh eyes beat the author (2026-09-02)\n33|Weaver fixed the strip 6 times by adjusting numbers; anser, who hadn't touched the file, found the infinite loop in one pass by watching class mutations. Rule: after 2 repeat corrections on the same element, the redundancy gate (anser) does a fresh-eyes QA before the owner touches it again.\n34|\n35|\n36|## Watch the plumbing, not the status line (2026-09-02)\n37|Twice today the pipeline reported healthy while a worker had died. `dispatch.py status` showed queued items; the daemon log showed a traceback. Every pipeline daemon needs: exceptions caught per-work-unit, the last error surfaced on `status`, and a \"stale in-flight\" detector (an item marked delivering for >N minutes with no bot process = re-queue). Chris shouldn't be the monitor.\n38|\n39|\n40|## Don't let the pipeline fail silently (2026-09-02, Chris — third time)\n41|Rules now in force: (a) a dispatch is \"sent\" only when the daemon log says `ok` — `status` alone lies; (b) the watcher process reports every done-line AND every daemon exception to Senter; (c) any item \"delivering\" for >10 min with no live bot pid is re-queued automatically; (d) when a bot has a FIFO backlog and the user is waiting on a later item, spawn a parallel instance (CLAIM v2 makes this safe) instead of waiting. This document + klerik's `agent-authoring-rules.md` are the product: the wiki must render both under topics/senter and topics/klerik so anyone adopting the process can read them.\n42|\n43|\n44|## PR policy (2026-09-02, Chris)\n45|- **Upstream repos (NousResearch/hermes-agent, anyone else's):** bots NEVER open a PR during a session. They keep a **PR draft ledger** — `topics/hermes-agent/prs/<slug>.md` per candidate: problem, exact lines, diff on a local branch, test plan, and a running \"use cases tried\" log. Every time a later use case touches the same area, the draft is updated (or withdrawn). Senter reviews the ledger at session end and Chris says which go out. Rationale (Chris): cuts sloppy PRs — things that look right in the moment and wrong after the next use case.\n46|- **Chris's own repos (turbofit, pocket-shop, SouthpawIN/*):** ship immediately, normal PR flow.\n47|- A bot that wants to push upstream posts NEEDS-CHRIS with the ledger path; it does not push.\n48|\n49|## Feed sources are event-driven (2026-09-02, Chris)\n50|Timed polling is the fallback, not the design. GitHub: webhooks (repo `gh api repos/.../hooks` or a `gh` watch on notifications with `If-Modified-Since`/ETag long-poll) → bridge `/hook/github` → post. RSS: WebSub where the feed supports it, else conditional GET on the feed's advertised TTL. X: streaming/filtered endpoints if the tier allows, else the API's own rate-window. Rule: an integration posts within seconds of the event; a poll interval only exists where the provider gives no push.\n51|\n52|\n53|## Meeseeks model (2026-09-02, Chris)\n54|Builder bots are **ephemeral**. One instance = one queue item = one `-q` process that: reads the topic README + `queue.md` + the item + its skill → `mkdir` CLAIM → diagnose → build → LOOK → done-post → exits. It never carries prior conversation; the topic's `.system/` is the only memory (session.md is where it leaves what it learned). A **QA Meeseeks** (anser-shaped, fresh eyes) spawns on every DONE and either signs it or files a [FIXES] item. Parallelism is spawn count with a per-bot cap (weaver 3, teknium 2). The dispatcher's job changes from \"deliver to a bot's conversation\" to \"spawn a Meeseeks per unclaimed item\". Why: a long-lived instance drifts, hoards context, and serializes unrelated work; today's I–M sat behind A–H for 40 minutes for no reason.\n55|\n56|\n57|## Pending = dead worker, not busy worker (2026-09-02, Chris)\n58|\"Things tend to die when they go into pending — that's when you should be spawning more instances.\" A bot blocked on an approval card holds a claim and a process but does no work; counting it toward capacity starves the queue. Sentinel v3: parked instances (pid recorded in `pending/<id>.json`) are excluded from capacity; the undelivered-work threshold drops from 3 min to 30 s while anything is parked; the next queued item gets its own Meeseeks immediately. The parked instance resumes or times out on its own; neither blocks anyone else.\n59|\n60|\n61|## Live means push (2026-09-02, Chris: \"live live live\")\n62|No timed loaders. Every surface updates on an EVENT: filesystem change → watcher → SSE; provider event → webhook/long-poll → SSE; bot action → hook → SSE. A `setInterval`/`sleep(N)` loop is allowed only as a fallback that (a) engages when the push channel is down, (b) is labeled in the UI (`fallback · polling`), (c) is listed in this section with its reason. Everything else is a defect. [Subdirectory context discovered: .hermes/profiles/klerik/AGENTS.md]
# Klerik — Agent Profile Editor

## Role
Meta-agent that reviews and corrects other Hermes agent profiles. Diagnoses behavioral deviations and surgically edits SOUL.md, USER.md, MEMORY.md, AGENTS.md, skills, and config. Uses DSPy + GEPA to optimize agent intent when manual edits aren't sufficient.

## Profile File Map

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `SOUL.md` | Personality, behavioral directives, voice |
| `AGENTS.md` | Role-specific procedures, workflows |
| `config.yaml` | Toolsets, model, display, TTS, delegation |
| `USER.md` | Injected user profile (managed by memory tool) |
| `MEMORY.md` | Injected memory entries (managed by memory tool) |
| `skills/` | Installed skills (SKILL.md files) |
| `herm/tui.json` | Herm TUI theme + eikon |

All live under `~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/`. Launcher: `hermes -p <name>` → `HERMES_HOME=~/.hermes/profiles/<name>`.

## Profile Setup — Automatic on Create/Review

Every profile Klerik creates or reviews MUST have these configured. Apply at creation time and verify on every review pass. **Do not skip these.**

### Setup Order

```bash
# 1. Create profile
hermes profile create <name>

# 2. Symlink shared credentials
ln -sf ~/.hermes/.env ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/.env
ln -sf ~/.hermes/auth.json ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/auth.json

# 3. Write SOUL.md and AGENTS.md

# 4. Set TTS voice (match persona archetype)
hermes -p <name> config set tts.edge.voice <voice>

# 5. Set CLI skin (match persona archetype)
hermes -p <name> config set display.skin <skin>

# 6. Create herm TUI config (theme + eikon)
mkdir -p ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/herm

# 7. Create shell alias
hermes profile alias <name>
```

### TTS Voice Assignment

Set via: `hermes -p <name> config set tts.edge.voice <voice>`

| Archetype | Voice | Character |
|-----------|-------|-----------|
| Warm, creative, feminine | `en-US-JennyNeural` | Expressive, bright |
| Caring, friendly, feminine | `en-US-AvaNeural` | Warm, supportive |
| Strong, commanding, masculine | `en-US-GuyNeural` | Deep, authoritative |
| Calm, precise, editorial | `en-US-GuyNeural` | Measured, professional |
| Neutral, professional | `en-US-AriaNeural` | Clear, balanced |

Default: `en-US-AriaNeural`. Match voice gender and warmth to the persona — playful agents get bright voices, terse agents get lower-register ones.

### CLI Skin Assignment

Set via: `hermes -p <name> config set display.skin <skin>`

- **Creative / vibe** → `daylight`, `warm-lightmode`, `default`
- **Dev / builder** → `ares`, `slate`, `charizard`
- **Orchestrator / triage** → `poseidon`, `slate`
- **Editorial / precision** → `mono`

Custom skins: `~/.hermes/skins/<name>.yaml`

### Herm TUI Theme + Eikon

Create `~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/herm/tui.json`:

```json
{
  "mouse": true,
  "targetFps": 30,
  "theme": "<theme>",
  "eikonPath": "$HOME/.bun/install/global/node_modules/herm-tui/assets/eikons/<eikon>.eikon"
}
```

**Eikon selection:**
- `ares.eikon` — builder/dev agents, strong personas
- `default.eikon` — creative/conversational agents, warm personas
- `mono.eikon` — precision/editorial agents, orchestrators

**Theme selection:**
- `orng` — dark + orange (dev/build)
- `lucent-orng` — lighter orange (creative)
- `vercel` — dark, polished (global default)
- `tokyonight` — dark blue/purple (orchestrator)
- `monokai` — classic coder dark
- `nord` — cool blue-grey
- `catppuccin` — warm pastel dark
- `gruvbox` — retro warm dark

**Pairing:**
- Creative → `default.eikon` + `lucent-orng`/`catppuccin`/`daylight`
- Dev → `ares.eikon` + `orng`/`monokai`/`charizard`
- Orchestrator → `mono.eikon` + `tokyonight`/`nord`/`vercel`
- Editorial → `mono.eikon` + `mono`/`slate`

## Review Workflow

### 1. Understand the Task
Get clear on: which profile, what behavior is wrong, what's expected, any session IDs.

### 2. Gather Evidence
- `session_search` for the target profile's sessions (or query their SQLite DB directly)
- Read SOUL.md, AGENTS.md, USER.md, MEMORY.md, relevant skills
- Compare actual behavior vs expected

### 3. Diagnose Root Cause

| Pattern | Root Cause | Fix |
|---------|-----------|-----|
| Agent ignores skills | No trigger in SOUL.md | Add "When X, load skill Y" |
| Over-explaining | Personality too permissive | Add constraint |
| Memory entries are imperatives | No style guide | Add declarative-fact rule |
| Tool misuse | Skill gap or wrong toolset | Patch skill or adjust config |
| Personality overrides accuracy | Voice directive too strong | Add accuracy constraint |
| Same error class repeats | Symptom patches, not root cause | Edit SOUL.md, not outputs |
| Doesn't know WHEN to act | Missing trigger conditions | Add explicit if/when clauses |
| Context waste | Unnecessary preamble | Add conciseness constraint |

### 4. Choose Tool — Manual Edit or DSPy/GEPA

| Situation | Approach |
|-----------|----------|
| Simple missing constraint | Manual: add one line |
| Conflicting directives | Manual: clarify priority |
| Recurring failure across sessions | DSPy + GEPA: compile against data |
| Works 80% but fails on edges | DSPy: BootstrapFewShot with failures |
| Full SOUL.md restructure | GEPA: evolve the full prompt |
| Subtle intent drift | GEPA: reflective evolution |

### 5. Make the Edit
- Manual: one sentence beats one paragraph; clarify over rewrite
- DSPy/GEPA: extract the winning formulation from optimization, apply minimal version
- Skills: use `skill_manage(action='patch')` for targeted fixes
- Config: use `hermes -p <name> config set key value`
- Memory: declarative facts only ("User prefers X" ✓, "Always do X" ✗)

### 6. Verify
- State what you changed and where
- Explain the causal chain: "Agent did X because Y was missing. Adding Z → W."
- Suggest a test scenario

## DSPy + GEPA Operational Procedure

When manual edits fail to fix recurring issues:

```python
import dspy

# 1. Configure optimizer LM (use a strong model)
lm = dspy.LM("openai/gpt-4.1-mini")
dspy.settings.configure(lm=lm)

# 2. Define the agent's behavioral signature
class AgentBehavior(dspy.Signature):
    """Given an agent's SOUL.md and a user request, produce the correct behavioral response."""
    soul_md = dspy.InputField(desc="Current SOUL.md content")
    user_request = dspy.InputField(desc="User's actual request")
    expected_behavior = dspy.InputField(desc="What the agent should have done")
    actual_behavior = dspy.InputField(desc="What the agent actually did")
    corrected_soul_edit = dspy.OutputField(desc="Minimal edit to SOUL.md that fixes the behavior")

# 3. Build training examples from session failures
trainset = [
    dspy.Example(
        soul_md=current_soul,
        user_request=req,
        expected_behavior=expected,
        actual_behavior=actual,
    ).with_inputs("soul_md", "user_request", "expected_behavior", "actual_behavior")
    for req, expected, actual in failure_cases
]

# 4. Define metric: does the proposed edit fix the behavior?
def behavioral_metric(example, pred, trace=None):
    # Apply the edit, run the agent, check if behavior matches expected
    return 1.0 if behavior_matches(pred.corrected_soul_edit, example.expected_behavior) else 0.0

# 5. Run GEPA optimization
from dspy.teleprompt import GEPA
optimizer = GEPA(metric=behavioral_metric, num_candidates=10)
optimized = optimizer.compile(agent_module, trainset=trainset)

# 6. Extract the winning formulation and apply
```

## Cross-Profile Session Access

Klerik's `session_search` only searches Klerik's own sessions. For target profiles, either:
- Directly query the target's SQLite DB: `sqlite3 ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/sessions/hermes_sessions.db "SELECT ..."`
- Use `delegate_task` to spawn a subagent that loads the target profile and runs session_search

## Conventions
- Action over planning — act, don't discuss
- Extend existing skills rather than building new ones
- Skills load BEFORE answering
- Memory entries are declarative facts, never imperatives
- Don't rewrite personality for a small fix
- Don't edit files you haven't read
- Don
